     │ Places where the specs disagree                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Category slug: the frontend URL example uses category=counter-drone, │
     │    but the API and backend use counter_drone. A request with the        │
     │    hyphenated value would get a 422. [Use counter_drone in the URL as   │
     │    well.]                                                               │
     │ 2. Required-to-publish list: frontend §4.6 omits state_name, but        │
     │    backend §4 rule 2 requires it when buyer_type=state. [Follow the     │
     │    backend; the frontend highlights state_name conditionally.]          │
     │ 3. Admin list default: the contract gives the admin list "same query    │
     │    params as the public list", and that includes status defaulting to   │
     │    open. The Admin Dashboard, however, shows all tenders, including     │
     │    closed ones. [The admin list defaults status to all.]                │
     │ 4. Dashboard states: the dashboard shows Draft/Published/Closed, but    │
     │    status_admin only filters draft/published. Closed comes from         │
     │    is_closed, and it is unclear whether Closed can be a draft. [Show    │
     │    Closed only for published + is_closed.]                              │
     │ 5. "Add tender" without a PDF: the user journey asks for manual entry,  │
     │    but the contract only allows creating a tender through PDF upload    │
     │    plus extraction. [Resolved: use the stub extractor.]                 │
     │ 6. Extraction reason and truncation: the backend says to record the "no │
     │    readable text" reason and that long text "was cut" in                │
     │    ExtractionRun, but the entity has no fields for either. [Add         │
     │    failure_reason and input_truncated when the real extractor lands.]   │
     │ 7. Outcome enums: ExtractionRun has timed_out, but                      │
     │    Tender.extraction_status doesn't. The mapping (timed_out → failed)   │
     │    is implied, not stated. [Map to failed.]                             │
     │ 8. Re-extract: the contract maps POST …/extract to the Review screen,   │
     │    but frontend §4.6 doesn't describe a re-extract button. (Deferred,   │
     │    but needs a decision.)                                               │
     │ 9. Open questions: the goal spec still lists 3 open questions, while    │
     │    the frontend, backend and contract all say "None at present".        │
     │ 10. Unpublish confirmation: the frontend flow says "Unpublish →         │
     │     confirm", but §4.4 doesn't list a confirmation text, unlike         │
     │     Publish. [Use "Unpublish this tender? It will be hidden from the    │
     │     public list."]                                                      │

     ~/.claude/plans/pasted-content-id-6c14-read-the-happy-raccoon.md · /plan to
     edit
     Build Plan — Slice 1: manual tender → publish → public list/detail (local)

     Context

     ~/projects/drone-tender-desk contains only specs/ on main, in a single
     commit (0bfee99) tracking origin/main. The first goal is the smallest
     end-to-end journey, without AI. The admin logs in, uploads a PDF with a
     source URL, fills in the fields by hand, and publishes. The tender then
     appears on the public Tender List and Detail pages. Everything runs locally
     against PostgreSQL in Docker.

     Decisions taken with the user
     - "By hand" stays inside the contract. POST /api/admin/tenders (multipart,
       PDF + source_url) calls an extraction-service stub that always returns
       failed. The draft comes back with every extractable field null. The admin
       fills in fields through PATCH and then publishes. Real Claude extraction
       later replaces only the stub.
     - Login and a minimal admin UI are in scope. JWT auth guards admin
       endpoints from day one.
     - All bracketed defaults are accepted, for both the open decisions and the
       spec disagreements. Additions: counter-drone includes detection-only
       systems; the re-extract button is left out of this slice; failure_reason
       and input_truncated are added to ExtractionRun now; the goal-spec Open
       Questions become Decisions.

     Deferred to later slices: the Claude call, POST …/extract and the
     re-extract button, GET …/pdf-url and the PDF pane, S3 (local folder for
     now), rate limiting, deployment (needs the Musafir / "Chapter 6" reference,
     still open), and the performance targets.

     Git: create branch slice-1 from main. The spec update is its first commit,
     followed by one commit per task. Nothing is pushed unless you ask.

     Tasks

     T0. Update the four specs with the decisions (one commit, no code)

     goal-spec.md
     - Replace Open Questions with Decisions Made:
       - OEM users cannot suggest tenders in v1.
       - One admin account.
       - Counter-drone covers detection-only systems (radars, RF detectors) as
         well as systems that stop a drone (jammers, interceptors).
     - Update the Glossary Category row to match.

     frontend-spec.md
     - §4.1: change the URL example to category=counter_drone. Add simple
       Prev/Next pagination (the API pages at 20).
     - §4.1/§7: "Closing soon" uses India-time (Asia/Kolkata) dates, the same as
       the backend.
     - §4.4: Closed is shown only for published tenders whose is_closed is true.
       Unpublish asks "Unpublish this tender? It will be hidden from the public
       list."
     - §4.6: add state_name as required when the buyer type is State.
     - §6 Log out: client-side only. The token is discarded and stays valid on
       the server until it expires.
     - New section Later Additions: a re-extract button on Review Tender (uses
       POST …/extract).
     - §1: VITE_API_BASE_URL does not include /api.

     backend-spec.md
     - §3 ExtractionRun: add failure_reason (text, nullable, e.g. "no readable
       text", "timeout", "invalid JSON") and input_truncated (boolean, default
       false).
     - §4 rule 10: search does a case-insensitive substring match (ILIKE), and
       every word must match (AND). To revisit if the 500 ms target is missed.
     - New rule: an ExtractionRun outcome of timed_out sets the Tender's
       extraction_status to failed.
     - §6/§9: add an UPLOAD_DIR env var. S3 is used when S3_BUCKET_NAME is set,
       otherwise the local folder. Name the stub extractor (model_name "stub",
       prompt_version "none").
     - Timestamps are returned in IST (+05:30).

     api-contract.md
     - §1: VITE_API_BASE_URL excludes /api. Timestamps are in IST. Search
       semantics are as in the backend spec.
     - §4 GET /api/admin/tenders: status defaults to all here, not open. The
       dashboard's Closed state means published + is_closed.
     - There is no logout endpoint; logout is client-side.
     - Check: git diff shows only specs/ changes. Each disagreement item (1–10
       below) is resolved in the text. No spec has an "Open Questions" section
       with open items. A single commit: "Record v0.2 spec decisions". Version
       lines are bumped to "Draft v0.2".

     T1. CLAUDE.md
     - Files: CLAUDE.md
     - Content:
       - specs/ is the source of truth. If code and specs disagree, stop and
         ask.
       - Tooling: Python 3.12 + uv, Node 20, TypeScript, monorepo with backend/
         and frontend/.
       - Commands to run the database, backend, frontend and tests.
       - Conventions: error envelope, null for empty fields, YYYY-MM-DD dates,
         is_closed computed in IST, no secrets in the frontend or logs, never
         invent extracted values.
       - Slice scope and the deferred list.
     - Check: the file exists. Every listed command is verified working at T11.

     T2. Repo skeleton + Postgres in Docker
     - Files: docker-compose.yml (postgres:16, a volume, a healthcheck, an extra
       _test database), .env.example (DATABASE_URL, JWT_SECRET,
       ALLOWED_ORIGINS, UPLOAD_DIR, blank ANTHROPIC_API_KEY/S3_BUCKET_NAME,
       VITE_API_BASE_URL), .gitignore
     - Check: docker compose up -d db starts the database, pg_isready reports
       OK, and both databases exist.

     T3. Backend skeleton + health + error envelope
     - Files: backend/pyproject.toml, app/main.py, config.py, db.py, errors.py
       (handlers that map to {error:{code,message,fields?}}, with FastAPI
       validation 422s reshaped into it), routers/health.py, tests/conftest.py,
       tests/test_health.py
     - Check: pytest passes. curl /api/health returns
       {"status":"ok","database":"ok"}. With the database down, the response is
       a non-200 in the envelope.

     T4. Data model + first migration
     - Files: app/models.py (Admin, Tender, and ExtractionRun including
       failure_reason and input_truncated), alembic/env.py,
       alembic/versions/0001_initial.py

      4  
      5  ## 1. Problem Statement
      6  Indian government RFIs and RFPs for drones and counter-drone sys
         tems are published in many separate places: the Ministry of Defe
         nce website, the GeM portal, the Central Public Procurement Port
         al, state police websites, PSUs and research institutes. Each on
         e is a long PDF in a different format. A drone or anti-drone OEM
          today has to check all these sites by hand, then read every doc
         ument to find the few facts that decide whether to bid: what is 
         being bought, how many, the key technical requirements, who is e
         ligible, and when bids close. Opportunities are missed and readi
         ng time is wasted.

      5  ## 1. General Rules
      6 -- **Base path:** all endpoints start with `/api`. The full addre
        -ss comes from `VITE_API_BASE_URL` in the frontend.              
      6 +- **Base path:** all endpoints start with `/api`. The server add
        +ress comes from `VITE_API_BASE_URL` in the frontend, which does 
        +not include `/api` (for example `http://localhost:8000`).       
      7  - **Format:** requests and responses are JSON, except the PDF up
         load, which is `multipart/form-data`.

         r example `"2026-11-14"`.

         ple `"2026-10-12T22:30:00+05:30"`.

       7  - **Format:** requests and responses are JSON, except the PDF u
          pload, which is `multipart/form-data`.
       8  - **Dates** (calendar days) are strings in `YYYY-MM-DD` form, f
          or example `"2026-11-14"`.
       9 -- **Timestamps** are ISO 8601 strings with a time zone, for exa
         -mple `"2026-10-12T22:30:00+05:30"`.                            
       9 +- **Timestamps** are ISO 8601 strings in India time (`+05:30`),
         + for example `"2026-10-12T22:30:00+05:30"`.                    
      10  - **Empty fields** are `null`, never an empty string and never 
          a made-up value.
      11  - **Admin endpoints** need the header `Authorization: Bearer <t
          oken>`.
      12  - **Open or Closed** is worked out by the backend (India time) 
          and sent as `is_closed`. The frontend never calculates it.

⏺ T3 is done and committed on slice-1 as 598f71b. It isn't pushed: your local
  branch is one commit ahead of GitHub.

  What was built (in backend/):
  - Project setup: a uv project pinned to Python 3.12, which uv downloaded
    (3.12.15). The system Python 3.14 is untouched.
  - app/config.py: reads settings from the repo-root .env.
  - app/db.py: sets up the database connection.
  - app/main.py: creates the app, with CORS allowed for the origins in
    ALLOWED_ORIGINS.
  - app/errors.py: turns every error into the contract's format: {"error": 
    {code, message, fields?}}. FastAPI's own validation errors are reshaped into
    it too, and unexpected errors return a general message while the details go
    only to the logs.
  - app/routers/health.py: GET /api/health, which checks the database answers.
  - Tests: tests/test_health.py and tests/test_errors.py. They run against
    tender_desk_test and refuse to start if TEST_DATABASE_URL is missing.

  Check results:
  - pytest: 6 tests pass, covering health with the database up and unreachable,
    plus 404, 422 and 500 responses in the error format.
  - Database up: curl /api/health returned {"status":"ok","database":"ok"} with
    a 200.
  - Database unreachable: a 500 in the error format with a general message. The
    real connection error appeared only in the server log. To simulate this I
    pointed a second server at a closed port rather than stopping your
    container, so Postgres is still running.
  - CORS: a request from http://localhost:5173 (the frontend dev server) is
    allowed.

  Things to know:
  - New .env: I created .env at the repo root from .env.example, with a randomly
    generated JWT_SECRET. Git ignores it, and it isn't committed.
  - First test run failed: it gave "Failed to spawn: pytest". A uv sync found
    nothing to change, and the next run worked. I didn't find the cause, but it
    hasn't come back.
  - Status codes the contract doesn't cover: for example 405 (wrong HTTP
    method). These return a bad_request code, which isn't in the contract's
    table. Tell me if you'd prefer something else, or I can add it to the
    contract.
  - Test warning: pytest shows a deprecation warning that FastAPI's test client
    will move from httpx to httpx2. It's harmless for now.

  Next is T4: the data model and the first Alembic migration.

⏺ T4 is done and committed on slice-1 as 45b65cc. It isn't pushed: your local
  branch is now two commits ahead of GitHub.

  What was built (in backend/):
  - app/models.py: the Admin, Tender and ExtractionRun tables from the backend
    spec. Enum columns are native Postgres types that hold exactly the contract
    values, for example counter_drone and RFI/RFP.
  - ExtractionRun: includes the failure_reason and input_truncated fields added
    to the spec in T0.
  - Alembic: alembic/env.py takes the database address from .env. The test suite
    can point it at the test database instead.
  - alembic/versions/0001_initial.py: the first migration, generated from the
    models. I fixed its downgrade, which originally left the enum types behind
    and would have stopped the migration from being re-applied.
  - tests/test_migrations.py: 5 new tests. The test database is now rebuilt from
    the real migration at the start of every test run.

  Check results:
  - pytest: all 11 tests pass, the 6 from T3 plus 5 new ones.
  - Dev database: alembic upgrade head worked on the clean database and it's now
    at 0001 (head). \d tenders, \d extraction_runs and \d admins match the
    spec, including the enum types, fields_not_found text[] and the
    time-zone-aware timestamps.
  - Round-trip: a downgrade left 0 tables and 0 enum types, and the upgrade
    rebuilt everything.
  - alembic check: "No new upgrade operations detected", so the models and the
    migration agree.

  Decisions I made where the spec says nothing:
  - Empty fields: every field the AI can extract may be empty, so drafts can
    hold empty fields. source_url is always required.
  - Always set: status (default draft), extraction_status and fields_not_found
    (default empty list).
  - May be empty: pdf_storage_key.
  - ExtractionRun: raw_output, input_tokens and output_tokens may be empty,
    since the stub returns nothing. duration_ms is always required.
  - Publishing rules live in the code, not the database: for example, the state
    being required for a State buyer. The service layer enforces them in T7.
  - Indexes: one on tender status + bid closing date, for the public list.

  Next is T5: admin authentication (password hashing, an 8-hour login token, the
  create-admin command, and POST /api/admin/login).

⏺ T5 is done and committed on slice-1 as 778996d. It isn't pushed: your local
  branch is now three commits ahead of GitHub.

  What was built (in backend/):
  - app/security.py:
    - Passwords are stored only as bcrypt hashes.
    - Login tokens are signed with JWT_SECRET and last 8 hours.
    - require_admin is the check every admin endpoint will use.
  - app/routers/admin_auth.py: POST /api/admin/login, which returns
    access_token, token_type and expires_at in India time.
  - app/cli.py: uv run python -m app.cli create-admin [--username admin]. It
    asks for the password twice and refuses to create a second admin.
  - app/timeutil.py: India-time helpers (now, today, timestamp formatting) that
    later tasks will reuse.
  - app/config.py: now requires a JWT_SECRET of at least 32 characters and won't
    start without one.
  - tests/test_auth.py: 26 new tests. Tables are now emptied after every test.

  Check results:
  - pytest: all 37 tests pass, the 11 from earlier plus 26 new ones.
  - Live check: I ran a real server against the dev database with a temporary
    smoke-test admin.
    - The command created the admin, and refused a second one with exit code 1.
    - The correct password returned a 200 with a token and expires_at 8 hours
      later in +05:30.
    - A wrong password and an unknown username both returned the same 401
      "Incorrect username or password."
    - The database holds only a $2b$ bcrypt hash, and the password never
      appeared in the server log.
  - Cleanup: I deleted the temporary admin. The dev database has 0 admins.

  Rejected token cases (each gives a 401):
  - no token
  - a malformed header
  - the wrong signing key
  - an unsigned token
  - a token with no expiry
  - an admin that no longer exists
  - an expired token, which also gets the message "Your session has expired.
    Please log in again."

  Decisions I made where the spec says nothing:
  - Password rules: at least 12 characters and at most 72 bytes, which is
    bcrypt's limit. A longer password is refused rather than silently cut short.
  - Unknown usernames: checked against a dummy hash, so a wrong username takes
    as long as a wrong password and the timing doesn't reveal which was wrong.
  - Scripted setup: ADMIN_PASSWORD can supply the password instead of the
    prompt. This is for the end-to-end test in T12.
  - Testing the guard: no admin endpoint exists yet, so the token tests use a
    temporary route inside the test file. Nothing extra was added to the app.
  - Deferred: limiting repeated login attempts (5 per minute) stays out of this
    slice, as planned.

  When you're ready: create your real admin with cd backend && uv run python -m 
  app.cli create-admin. You'll need it by T11.

  Next is T6: uploading a PDF to create a draft, with the stub extractor.

⏺ T6 is done and committed on slice-1 as 9a250f5. It isn't pushed: your local
  branch is now four commits ahead of GitHub.

  What was built (in backend/):
  - POST /api/admin/tenders (app/routers/admin_tenders.py): takes a PDF and a
    source URL, saves the PDF, creates a draft and runs extraction. It returns
    201 with the full admin view of the tender, as the contract describes.
  - app/extraction/service.py: the stub extractor, which always reports failed
    with the reason "extraction not enabled". It's built so the real Claude
    extractor can replace it later without changing anything else. If an
    extractor crashes or times out, the draft is still created and the run is
    recorded as failed. An ExtractionRun record is saved every time.
  - app/storage.py: saves PDFs to UPLOAD_DIR. A relative path is taken from the
    repo root, so the folder is ./uploads, which git ignores. Setting
    S3_BUCKET_NAME stops the app with a clear "not built yet" error.
  - app/schemas.py: the three response shapes from the contract. is_closed is
    worked out from today's date in India time, and timestamps are returned in
    +05:30.
  - Tests: tests/test_admin_create.py has 28 new tests. Uploads during tests go
    to a temporary folder, never the real one.

  Check results:
  - pytest: all 65 tests pass, the 37 from earlier plus 28 new ones.
  - Live upload checks (real server, dev database):

  ┌───────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │    Upload     │                         Result                          │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Valid PDF     │ 201: draft, extraction_status: "failed", all 13         │
  │               │ extractable fields null and listed in fields_not_found  │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Text file     │ 422 on the file field                                   │
  │ renamed .pdf  │                                                         │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 20 MB + 1     │ 413 file_too_large                                      │
  │ byte          │                                                         │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ ftp:// source │ 422 on the source_url field                             │
  │  URL          │                                                         │
  ├───────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ No login      │ 401                                                     │
  │ token         │                                                         │
  └───────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  - Database and disk: the database held one draft and one stub ExtractionRun,
    and the PDF was saved under uploads/tenders/. Rejected uploads left no file
    and no database row.
  - Cleanup: I deleted the temporary admin, the test tenders and runs, and the
    uploaded files. The dev database is empty again and uploads/ is gone.

  Bug found and fixed during the live check: the server log didn't show the
  "Extraction outcome" line that the backend spec requires, because uvicorn only
  sets up logging for its own messages. app/main.py now sends the app's own log
  lines to the server log. A new test checks this, and the line now appears on
  the real server.

  Decisions I made where the spec says nothing:
  - PDF check: the file's content must contain %PDF- in its first 1024 bytes,
    which the PDF format allows. The file name and the browser's stated file
    type are ignored.
  - Source URL: must be http or https with a host and no spaces, and at most
    2048 characters. Spaces at either end are trimmed.
  - Failed save: if saving to the database fails after the PDF is stored, the
    PDF is deleted so no orphan file is left behind.
  - Extractor output: only extractable fields are kept from the extractor's
    result, so unknown keys are dropped. Checking dates and enum values belongs
    to the real extractor in a later slice.

  Next is T7: the admin endpoints to read, edit, publish and unpublish tenders.

⏺ T7 is done and committed on slice-1 as 2d1ed25. It isn't pushed: your local
  branch is now five commits ahead of GitHub.

  What was built (in backend/):
  - Admin endpoints (app/routers/admin_tenders.py):
    - GET /api/admin/tenders: every tender, drafts included. status defaults to
      all, and the list supports status_admin, search, filters and paging.
    - GET /api/admin/tenders/{id}.
    - PATCH /api/admin/tenders/{id}.
    - POST /api/admin/tenders/{id}/publish and POST 
      /api/admin/tenders/{id}/unpublish.
  - app/services/listing.py:
    - Search matches every word, and each word can match any of title, issuing
      body, item and technical requirements.
    - Filters cover category, document type, buyer type and open/closed in India
      time.
    - Sort order is open soonest first, then closed most recent first.
    - The plan had this in T8. I built it now because the admin list needs it,
      and T8's public list will reuse it.
  - app/services/tenders.py: the publish rules, the edit rules, and the
    publish/unpublish status changes.
  - Tests: tests/test_admin_lifecycle.py and tests/test_admin_list.py, with 55
    new tests.

  Check results:
  - pytest: all 120 tests pass, the 65 from earlier plus 55 new ones.
  - Every plan check is covered by a test:
    - Invalid PATCH: 422 and nothing saved.
    - Publishing with missing fields: 422 listing all of them, including
      state_name for a State buyer.
    - Valid publish: published_at set.
    - Publish twice: 409.
    - Unpublish: back to draft.
    - Unpublish a draft: 409.
    - Blanking a required field on a published tender: 422.
    - Every admin route without a token: 401.
  - Live check on a real server, run as one flow:
    a. Upload a PDF, which created a draft.
    b. Publish the empty draft: 422 listing the 6 missing fields.
    c. Fill in the fields with PATCH.
    d. Publish: published_at set in +05:30.
    e. Publish again: 409.
    f. Remove the title from the published tender: 422.
    g. The admin list showed the tender as published.
    h. Unpublish: back to draft.
    i. Unpublish again: 409.
  - Cleanup: the dev database and uploads/ are empty again.

  Decisions I made where the spec says nothing:
  - Blank text: text sent as "" or spaces is saved as null, matching the
    contract's "never an empty string". Spaces at either end are trimmed.
  - Dates: must be exactly YYYY-MM-DD and a real calendar date. 2026-02-30,
    14/11/2026, timestamps and numbers are all refused.
  - Unknown fields: PATCH refuses unknown fields with a 422, including status
    and fields_not_found, rather than silently ignoring them.
  - State with a Central buyer: a state name with a Central buyer is refused.
    While the buyer type is still empty, a state name is allowed so a draft can
    be filled in any order.
  - fields_not_found: PATCH leaves it unchanged. It records what the AI couldn't
    find, not what is empty now.
  - Unpublish: clears published_at, and publishing again sets a new time.
  - Already closed: a tender whose closing date has passed can still be
    published, and it shows as Closed. Past tenders are useful to OEMs.
  - Undated drafts: in the admin list, a draft with no closing date counts as
    open and is listed after the dated open tenders.

  Next is T8: the public endpoints GET /api/tenders and GET /api/tenders/{id},
  with the date-based tests.

⏺ T8 is done and committed on slice-1 as c7220f4. It isn't pushed: your local
  branch is now six commits ahead of GitHub. This completes the backend for
  slice 1.

  What was built (in backend/):
  - app/routers/public_tenders.py:
    - GET /api/tenders: published tenders only, open by default, with search,
      filters, sorting and paging. It reuses the listing code from T7.
    - GET /api/tenders/{id}: the public detail view. A draft gets exactly the
      same 404 response as an id that doesn't exist.
  - app/timeutil.py: "today" (India time) for the public endpoints now comes
    through a setting tests can override, so they freeze it at 14 Nov 2026.
  - tests/test_public.py: 51 new tests.

  Check results:
  - pytest: all 171 tests pass, the 120 from earlier plus 51 new ones. The first
    run had 38 errors, all caused by a mistake in my test setup (a field passed
    twice), not by the app. After fixing it, everything passed.
  - The plan's checks, each covered by a test:
    - A draft never appears in the list under open, closed or all, and returns
      404 by id with the same response as an unknown id.
    - A tender closing today is still Open, and one that closed yesterday is
      Closed and only listed under closed or all.
    - The sort order is correct for open, closed and all.
    - Every filter works, alone and combined.
    - A multi-word search requires every word and finds text in
      technical_requirements.
    - category=helicopter and page_size=101 both return 422.
  - Other things the tests cover:
    - India time vs UTC: at 20:00 UTC on 13 Nov, India time is already 14 Nov.
    - Search ignores case, and %, _ and \ are matched literally rather than as
      wildcards.
    - The public responses contain no admin-only fields.
    - An empty result is a 200, not an error.
    - Unpublishing removes a tender from the public endpoints at once.
  - Live check on a real server:
    a. I published one tender and kept a second as a draft.
    b. /api/tenders without logging in showed only the published one.
    c. A search for a word only in the draft returned nothing.
    d. The detail view showed the full public fields.
    e. The draft and an unknown id both returned the same 404.
    f. category=helicopter returned 422.
    g. After unpublishing, the tender disappeared from the list and its detail
       returned 404.
  - Cleanup: the dev database and uploads/ are empty again.

  Decision I made where the spec says nothing: search terms longer than 200
  characters are refused with a 422.

  Router, React Query), the API client and the date formatting helpers.

  slice-1 is now pushed to GitHub: the seven commits from T3 to T9 went up, and
  your local branch matches GitHub.

  T9 had finished all its checks before the drop:
  - Tests: 33 frontend tests pass.
  - Build and lint: both pass. I removed an unused export from App.tsx that the
    linter flagged.
  - No secrets in the build: the built frontend contains none of the backend's
    secrets.

    requests from http://localhost:5173 but not from other sites.

  One check wasn't done: I haven't looked at the page in a real browser. The
  header is only covered by a component test. I'll do a browser check once the
  real pages exist, in T10 or at T12.

  A few things worth knowing about T9:
  - Versions: the Vite template brought newer versions than the plan assumed:
    React 19, Vite 8, TypeScript 6, Tailwind 4 and React Router 8.
  - Blocked install script: npm blocked one install script, for fsevents, an
    optional macOS file watcher that works without it. I left it blocked.
  - Shared .env: the frontend reads the repo-root .env. Only variables starting
    with VITE_ reach the browser.

  main on GitHub still lacks the spec commit 51abb67. Next is T10: the public
  Tender List and Detail pages.

  Command-line checks, with the backend and Vite running:
  - Reload: the dev server returns the app page for /,
    /?category=counter_drone&status=all, /tenders/1, /tenders/3 and an unknown
    path, so refreshing on any of them works.
  - List requests: the default view returns only the open tender.
    category=counter_drone&status=all returns the open and the closed tender.
    q=radar jammer finds the tender whose requirements mention both words.
    category=drone returns nothing, because the only drone tender is a draft.
  - CORS: the backend accepts requests from http://localhost:5173.
  - Detail requests: tender 1 is open, tender 2 is closed with state Punjab, and
    the draft (3) returns "Tender not found."
  - Logs: no errors in either server's log.

  It passed once re-run.

  Browser steps for you: open http://localhost:5173. Today in India is 13 Oct
  2026.

  1. /: one tender, "Procurement of Counter Drone Systems". It shows Border
     Security Force, RFP, Counter-drone, Central, "Closes 17 Oct 2026", and the
     badges Open and Closing soon.
  2. Status → All: the URL becomes /?status=all and "Portable anti-drone
     jammers" appears below it, marked Closed, with "State · Punjab".
  3. Category → Counter-drone, then reload: the URL
     /?category=counter_drone&status=all survives the reload, and both menus
     keep their values.
  4. Search radar jammer and press Enter: only the BSF tender is listed. Then
     search uav: "No tenders match these filters" with a Clear filters button,
     which resets to /.
  5. Click the BSF tender:
     - Every field appears, with dates written out ("1 Oct 2026", "17 Oct
       2026").
     - The requirements show on two lines, and the summary notice is shown.
     - "View original document" opens in a new tab. The example.gov.in address
       isn't real, so that page won't load.
  6. "← Back to tenders": you return to the list with the filters you had.
  7. /tenders/2: has a State row (Punjab). Empty fields say "Not stated in the
     document."
  8. /tenders/3 (the draft) and /tenders/abc: both show "Tender not found".
  9. Phone width (375 px, using your browser's device mode): the rows and
     filters fit without scrolling sideways.
  10. Error state: stop the backend with pkill -f "uvicorn app.main:app --port 
      8000" and reload /. You should see "Could not load tenders. Please try
      again." with a Retry button. Restart the backend with cd backend && uv run
      uvicorn app.main:app --port 8000, then click Retry and the list loads.

  Still running: both servers. The dev database holds the sample data (3
  tenders, 1 PDF each in uploads/) and a temporary smoke-test admin. None of it
  is in git.

❯ Continue with T11, then commit it on slice-1                                  

⏺ Starting T11, the admin side of the frontend. First the session layer:
  - the token is saved in the browser with its expiry time;
  - an auth context gives pages the token, login and logout;
  - any admin request that gets a 401 ends the session, which sends you to the
    login page with "Your session has expired. Please log in again." The login
    request itself is exempt.

⏺ T11 is committed on slice-1 as 2e27de3. I haven't pushed it, since you didn't
  ask this time, so your local branch is one commit ahead of GitHub.

  What was built (in frontend/src/):
  - Login and session: the token is saved in the browser with its expiry time.
    - Every /admin page sends a visitor who isn't logged in to /admin/login,
      then back to the page they asked for.
    - If an admin request gets a 401, or the saved token has expired, the
      session ends and the login page says "Your session has expired. Please log
      in again."
    - A wrong password at login shows only the normal error, not the "session
      expired" message.
  - Header: Admin dashboard and Logout links appear when logged in. Logout
    returns to the public list.
  - Dashboard: every tender, shown as Draft, Published or Closed. Publish
    appears on drafts only and Unpublish on published tenders only, each with
    its confirmation message. A refused publish names the missing fields.
  - Add Tender: the file must actually be a PDF (its content is checked, not
    just its name) and the source URL must be valid before Extract is enabled.
    While uploading it shows "Reading the document. This can take up to a
    minute.", then opens Review.
  - Review Tender:
    - Every field can be edited, and fields the AI didn't find are marked "Not
      found in document."
    - Empty required fields are highlighted, and Publish stays disabled until
      they're filled. The State field only appears, and is only required, for a
      State buyer.
    - Publish asks for confirmation, then saves what's on screen before
      publishing.
    - If a save fails, everything you typed stays and the problem field is
      shown.
  - Not included, as planned: the re-extract button and the PDF pane beside the
    form.

  Check results:
  - Frontend tests: all 100 pass, 34 of them new.
  - Type-check, linter and build: all clean.
  - Running servers: both are healthy, every admin route and page module loads
    through Vite, and neither log has errors.

  Bug found by a test and fixed: clicking Logout on an admin page landed on the
  login page instead of the public list. Clearing the token made the admin guard
  redirect before the navigation finished. Logout now waits for the navigation
  to finish before ending the session.

  Before your browser check: I deleted my temporary smoke-test admin. The app
  allows only one admin and I no longer had its random password. The three
  sample tenders are still there. Create your own admin with:
  ! cd backend && uv run python -m app.cli create-admin

  Browser steps at http://localhost:5173:
  1. Logged out, open /admin/tenders/2/review: you're sent to the login page.
     Log in with a wrong password: "Incorrect username or password.", the
     password is cleared and the username kept. Log in correctly: you land back
     on tender 2's review page.
  2. Header: shows Admin dashboard and Logout.
  3. Dashboard (/admin):
     - The BSF tender shows as Published, the Punjab tender as Closed, and the
       draft as Draft.
     - Unpublish the BSF tender and confirm. It changes to Draft, and / in
       another tab no longer lists it.
     - Publish it again.
  4. Add tender:
     - Extract stays disabled until you choose a file and enter a valid URL.
     - Choose a non-PDF renamed to .pdf: "Please choose a PDF file".
     - Choose a real PDF and https://example.gov.in/test.pdf, then Extract. You
       see the reading message, then Review with the "Automatic extraction did
       not work…" notice and every field marked "Not found in document."
  5. Review:
     - Required fields are red and Publish is disabled. Fill them in and Publish
       becomes enabled.
     - Set Buyer type to State: the State field appears and is required.
     - Publish and confirm: "Published…", and the tender appears on /.
  6. Save errors: stop the backend (pkill -f "uvicorn app.main:app --port 
     8000"), edit a field and click Save. You get an error and your edits stay.
     Restart the backend with cd backend && uv run uvicorn app.main:app --port 
     8000.
  7. Expired session: in your browser's developer tools, delete the
     drone-tender-desk.admin-session entry under Local Storage, then click
     anything on the dashboard. You're sent to login.
  8. Logout: you land on /.
  9. Phone width (375 px): the dashboard table scrolls inside its box, and the
     Review form stacks into one column.

  end, and running every command in CLAUDE.md from a clean clone.

  Browser steps (http://localhost:5173)

  1. Login and the guard
  1. While logged out, open http://localhost:5173/admin/tenders/2/review. You
     should land on Admin login.
  2. Enter your username and a wrong password. Expect "Incorrect username or
     password.", an empty password box, and your username still filled in.
  3. Log in correctly. You should go back to tender 2's review page, not the
     dashboard.
  4. The header now shows Admin dashboard and Logout.

  2. Dashboard (/admin)
  1. There are three rows:
     - "Procurement of Counter Drone Systems" is Published, closing 17 Oct 2026.
     - "Portable anti-drone jammers" is Closed.
     - "Unpublished draft surveillance UAV" is Draft.
  2. Drafts show Publish. Published and closed tenders show Unpublish. Every row
     has Review/Edit.
  3. Click Publish on the draft and confirm. Expect a red message listing the
     missing fields: Issuing body, Buyer type, Document type and Bid closing
     date. The draft stays a Draft.
  4. Click Unpublish on the BSF tender. The confirmation reads "Unpublish this
     tender? It will be hidden from the public list." Click Cancel and nothing
     changes. Do it again and confirm: it changes to Draft, and / in another tab
     no longer lists it.

  3. Review an existing draft
  1. Open Review/Edit on the BSF tender, which is now a draft.
  2. All its fields are filled in and Publish is enabled.
  3. Clear Title. It turns red with "Required to publish." and Publish becomes
     disabled. Type the title back.
  4. Change Buyer type to State. A State field appears, red and required. Type
     Punjab. Then change the buyer type back to Central and the State field
     disappears.
  5. Click Publish and confirm "Publish this tender? It will be visible to
     everyone." Expect "Published. The tender is now on the public list." The
     buttons change to just Save, with a note that changes are public
     immediately.
  6. Change EMD and click Save. Expect "Saved.", and the public detail page
     /tenders/1 shows the new EMD.

  4. Add a tender
  1. On the dashboard, click Add tender. Extract is disabled.
  2. Choose fake.pdf. Expect "Please choose a PDF file", and Extract stays
     disabled.
  3. Choose sample.pdf. Type ftp://x as the source URL: an error appears and
     Extract stays disabled. Change it to https://example.gov.in/test.pdf and
     Extract becomes enabled.
  4. Click Extract. You'll see "Reading the document…" only for a moment,
     because the stub responds instantly.
  5. You land on Review tender for the new draft, which shows:
     - the amber notice "Automatic extraction did not work for this document.
       Please fill in the fields by hand."
     - every field empty and marked Not found in document.
     - the required fields in red, with Publish disabled
     - the Source URL already filled in
  6. Fill in the required fields and the marker disappears from each one you
     fill. Use the date picker for the closing date, then publish.

  5. Failed save keeps your edits
  1. Open any draft's review page and type something in a few fields.
  2. Stop the backend: ! pkill -f "uvicorn app.main:app --port 8000"
  3. Click Save draft. Expect "Could not reach the server. Your changes are
     still here; please try again." Everything you typed is still there.
  4. Start the backend again: ! cd backend && uv run uvicorn app.main:app --port
     8000. Then click Save draft and expect "Saved."

  6. Expired session
  1. Open the browser's developer tools, go to Application, then Local Storage,
     then http://localhost:5173, and delete drone-tender-desk.admin-session.
  2. Click Admin dashboard in the header. Expect the login page with "Your 
     session has expired. Please log in again." Log in again and you return to
     the dashboard.

  7. Logout
  1. Click Logout. You land on the public list /, and the admin links disappear.
  2. Open /admin. You're sent to the login page.

  8. Phone width
  1. Switch to device mode in developer tools, at 375 px wide.
  2. The dashboard table scrolls sideways inside its own box, not the whole
     page.
  3. The Review form is a single column, and the buttons wrap onto new lines.

  If you stop the backend in step 5 and leave it stopped, the other pages will
  show their error states. Restart it with the command in step 5.4. Tell me what
  you find, and whether you'd like me to push T11.

❯ New stage: deploy the app publicly, at zero cost. No paid services and no     
  credit card.                                                                  
                                                                                
  Target:                                                                       
  - Frontend on Vercel (Hobby plan).                                            
  - Backend (FastAPI) on Render's free web service.                             
  - PostgreSQL on Neon's free plan.                                             
  - AI extraction stays as the stub for now (no API key). Manual entry is the   
  live behaviour.                                                               
                                                                                
  Render's free plan loses saved files on restart and we won't use S3, so       
  propose storing uploaded PDFs in the database instead (20 MB limit stays).    
                                                                                
  Plan first, no code:                                                          
  1. List the spec changes needed (backend storage, deployment section, env     
  vars), as one task to do before anything else.                                
  2. List the accounts I must create and what I must do by hand in each         
  website, step by step for a beginner.

⏺ This plan depends on the current free-tier terms of Vercel, Render and Neon,
  which change often. I'll check them before planning around them.

⏺ Fetch(https://render.com/docs/free)
  ⎿  Received 363KB (200 OK)

⏺ Fetch(https://neon.com/docs/introduction/plans)
  ⎿  Received 44KB (200 OK)

⏺ Fetch(https://vercel.com/docs/plans/hobby)
  ⎿  Received 11.8KB (200 OK)

⏺ I checked the three providers' own pages before writing this. Five findings
  shape the plan:

  1. Vercel Hobby is for "non-commercial, personal use only". That's in Vercel's
     fair-use rules. If OEMs will use the site for business, that may break
     Vercel's terms, so it needs your decision (D1 below).
  2. Render's free service sleeps after 15 minutes without traffic, and waking 
     takes "about one minute". It also has no shell access. So migrations must
     run when the service starts, and the admin account has to be created from
     your own computer.
  3. Render's disk is wiped on every restart. Your idea of storing PDFs in the
     database is the right fix.
  4. Neon's free plan gives 1 GB of storage per project, and the database pauses
     after 5 minutes idle. At the 20 MB limit that's about 50 PDFs. Typical 1–5
     MB tenders fit in the hundreds, enough for the ~20 PDFs planned for v1.
  5. Neon gives connection strings starting postgresql://. Our app uses the
     psycopg driver, which needs postgresql+psycopg://, so the backend must
     convert the address or it won't start.

  ---

  Decisions I need from you first

  #: D1
  Decision: Vercel Hobby forbids commercial use. Is this a commercial service?
  My recommendation: If it's a personal or early prototype, use Vercel. If it's
  commercial, use another free host for the frontend (for example Cloudflare
  Pages). I'd check that host's free terms first.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D2
  Decision: The backend spec requires daily database backups. Neon's free plan
  has no scheduled backups I could confirm.
  My recommendation: Accept for now and write it down as a known gap. Optionally

  add a pg_dump command you run by hand weekly.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D3
  Decision: Region
  My recommendation: Singapore for both Render and Neon (closest to India),
  unless Neon offers Mumbai when you sign up.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D4
  Decision: Live testing will create real records, and the specs allow only
  unpublishing, not deleting.
  My recommendation: Use one real, public tender PDF for the live check, so
  nothing fake is ever published.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D5
  Decision: Local development storage
  My recommendation: Store PDFs in the database locally too, so there's one code

  path. UPLOAD_DIR is removed. Your current local sample tenders lose their
  files, which is fine because it's throwaway data.

  ---

  1. Spec changes, one commit before anything else (task P0)

  New specs/deployment-spec.md. The backend spec already promised one ("details
  go in a later deployment spec"). It covers:
  - the hosting map: Vercel serves the frontend, Render runs the backend, Neon
    holds the database;
  - region, free-plan limits and the cold start;
  - how migrations run (on every start) and how the admin is created (from a
    local terminal, with a password prompt);
  - the environment variables per host, and which are secrets;
  - the rule that live data is never seeded;
  - the go-live checks, and the known gaps (backups, the commercial-use
    question).

  goal-spec.md §8
  - Hosting changes from "backend on AWS, same as Musafir" to Render + Neon at
    zero cost.
  - AWS is noted as a possible later move.

  backend-spec.md
  - §1, §6 storage: PDFs are stored in PostgreSQL in their own tender_pdfs
    table: tender_id (unique), content (bytea), size_bytes, sha256, created_at.
    They're kept out of the tenders table so lists never load file contents.
  - §3 data model: Tender.pdf_storage_key is removed. S3 becomes "possible
    later".
  - §6 PDF viewing link: the backend signs its own 15-minute link
    (/api/admin/tenders/{id}/pdf?sig=…) instead of an S3 link. This is spec
    only; the PDF pane is still not built.
  - §7: rate limits are kept in memory, which suits one server; they reset when
    the server restarts. Production must run over HTTPS, and the API docs pages
    are turned off.
  - §9 environment variables:
    - remove UPLOAD_DIR and S3_BUCKET_NAME;
    - add ENABLE_API_DOCS;
    - DATABASE_URL may start with postgresql://, and the backend adds the
      +psycopg driver part itself;
    - "AWS / Terraform / GitHub Actions" is replaced by a pointer to
      deployment-spec.md.

  api-contract.md
  - pdf-url returns the backend's own signed link. The response shape is
    unchanged.
  - The production base URL is configured, never hardcoded.

  frontend-spec.md
  - New loading state: if loading takes more than 5 seconds, show "Starting up
    the server. This can take up to a minute."
  - A 429 from login shows its own message.

  Check: only files under specs/ change; every decision above appears in the
  text; a single commit.

  2. Accounts and what you do by hand

  You already have GitHub. You'll create three free accounts, signing up with 
  your GitHub account each time. As far as I could confirm, none asks for a card
  for these free plans. If any of them asks for a card, stop and tell me.

  ▎ Secret safety rule for these steps: paste secrets only into the website 
  ▎ forms or into your own Terminal window. Never paste them into this chat, 
  ▎ into !  commands here (those end up in the transcript), or into any file in 
  ▎ the repo.

  A. Neon (database), about 10 minutes

  1. Go to neon.com, click Sign up, then Continue with GitHub.
  2. Create a project:
     - name drone-tender-desk;
     - Postgres version 16, the same as local;
     - region Asia Pacific (Singapore), or Mumbai if listed;
     - database name tender_desk.
  3. On the project dashboard, click Connect.
     - Turn the Connection pooling switch off. We want the direct connection.
     - Copy the connection string. It looks like postgresql://user:password@ep-x
       xxx.ap-southeast-1.aws.neon.tech/tender_desk?sslmode=require.
  4. Save it in your password manager as "Neon DATABASE_URL". This is a secret.

  B. Render (backend), about 15 minutes

  1. Go to render.com, then Get Started, then GitHub.
     - When GitHub asks for access, choose Only select repositories, then
       drone-tender-desk.
  2. Make a JWT secret in your own Terminal:
  python3 -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(48))"
     Save it in your password manager as "Prod JWT_SECRET". Use a new value, not
     the one in your local .env.
  3. In the dashboard, click New +, then Web Service, then pick the repo. Fill
     in:
     - Name: drone-tender-desk-api. Your address becomes
       https://drone-tender-desk-api.onrender.com, or similar if the name is
       taken.
     - Region: Singapore. Branch: main. Root Directory: backend. Runtime:
       Python.
     - Build Command and Start Command: I'll give you the exact text in task T5.
     - Instance Type: Free.
  4. Under Environment Variables, add:
     - DATABASE_URL: paste the Neon string;
     - JWT_SECRET: paste the new secret;
     - ALLOWED_ORIGINS: https://placeholder.invalid for now, replaced in step D;
     - PYTHON_VERSION: 3.12.15;
     - ENABLE_API_DOCS: false.
  5. Under Advanced, set Health Check Path to /api/health, then click Create Web
     Service.
  6. Wait for the log to say "Live". Open
     https://<your-service>.onrender.com/api/health and you should see
     {"status":"ok","database":"ok"}.

  C. Vercel (frontend), about 10 minutes

  1. Go to vercel.com, then Sign Up, choose Hobby, then Continue with GitHub.
     - Again choose Only select repositories, then drone-tender-desk.
  2. Click Add New…, then Project, then Import the repo.
  3. Root Directory: click Edit and choose frontend. The framework should show
     Vite automatically.
  4. Under Environment Variables, add VITE_API_BASE_URL =
     https://<your-service>.onrender.com, with no /api and no trailing slash.
  5. Click Deploy. Note your address, for example
     https://drone-tender-desk.vercel.app.

  D. Connect the two (Render), 2 minutes

  1. In Render, open your service, then Environment, and set ALLOWED_ORIGINS to
     your exact Vercel address (https://drone-tender-desk.vercel.app).
  2. Click Save, rebuild and deploy.

  E. Create the live admin (your own Terminal), 3 minutes

  Render's free plan has no shell, so you create the admin from your computer.
  The password prompt stops the password from reaching your shell history.
  cd ~/projects/drone-tender-desk/backend
  read -s "DATABASE_URL?Paste the Neon DATABASE_URL, then Enter: " && export 
  DATABASE_URL
  uv run python -m app.cli create-admin --username <your-username>
  unset DATABASE_URL
  The command will show which database host it's about to write to and ask you
  to confirm (added in task T6). Store the admin password in your password
  manager.

  3. Public config vs secrets

  Setting: DATABASE_URL (Neon)
  Kind: Secret
  Where it lives: Render environment variables; your password manager; your
  Terminal only during step E
  Notes: Never in .env, the repo, the chat or the transcripts
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: JWT_SECRET (prod)
  Kind: Secret
  Where it lives: Render environment variables; your password manager
  Notes: A new value, different from local
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: Admin password
  Kind: Secret
  Where it lives: Your password manager only (the database stores only a bcrypt
  hash)
  Notes: Typed at a prompt, never on the command line
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: ALLOWED_ORIGINS
  Kind: Public config
  Where it lives: Render environment variables
  Notes: Your exact Vercel address
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: PYTHON_VERSION, ENABLE_API_DOCS
  Kind: Public config
  Where it lives: Render environment variables
  Notes:
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: VITE_API_BASE_URL
  Kind: Public config
  Where it lives: Vercel environment variables
  Notes: It's built into the JavaScript bundle, so anyone can see it. That's
  fine.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: Build/start commands, vercel.json
  Kind: Public config
  Where it lives: The repo
  Notes:
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Setting: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
  Kind: (future secret)
  Where it lives: Not set
  Notes: The stub needs no key

  Protection against committing secrets:
  - .env is already ignored by git.
  - Task T7 adds a check that fails if anything that looks like a Neon address
    or a token is staged.
  - Before every commit I'll show you the list of files being committed.

  4. Code tasks (small, each with a check)

  Task: T1 PDFs in the database
  What: TenderPdf model and migration 0002: create tender_pdfs, drop
  pdf_storage_key. Save the PDF in the same transaction as the tender, so a
  failed save leaves nothing behind. Remove LocalStorage, UPLOAD_DIR and
  S3_BUCKET_NAME, and update .env.example, the README and CLAUDE.md.
  Check: pytest: the stored bytes and sha256 match the upload; 20 MB is accepted

  and 20 MB + 1 gets a 413; a failed save leaves no row; listing doesn't load
  file contents. The migration round-trips. A live upload against local Docker
  shows the right size in tender_pdfs.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task: T2 Production settings
  What: Convert postgresql:// to postgresql+psycopg://. Add ENABLE_API_DOCS
  (default on locally) to turn /docs and /openapi.json off. Refuse to start if
  ALLOWED_ORIGINS contains *.
  Check: Unit tests for the address conversion. With docs disabled, /docs
  returns
  a 404 in the error format. Startup fails with a clear message when JWT_SECRET
   is missing.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task: T3 Rate limits (deferred in slice 1, but needed before going public)
  What: In memory: 5 login attempts per minute per IP, and 60 public requests
  per
  minute per IP, not counting /api/health. The client IP is read from Render's
  proxy header.
  Check: pytest: the 6th login gets a 429 in the error format; the 61st list
  request gets a 429; health is never limited; another IP isn't affected.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task: T4 Frontend for hosting
  What: Add vercel.json so every route serves index.html, which makes deep links

  and reloads work. Show the "Starting up the server…" message after 5 seconds
  of loading. Show a clear message for a 429 on login.
  Check: Vitest with fake timers: the message appears after 5 s and disappears
  when data arrives. The login 429 message has a test. Build and lint are
  clean.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task: T5 Render start script
  What: backend/render-start.sh runs alembic upgrade head, then uvicorn on $PORT

  with proxy headers on. I'll give exact build and start commands for the
  Render form.
  Check: Run the script locally with PORT=8100 against Docker: the migration
  runs, /api/health is OK, and running it a second time is harmless.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task: T6 Live-data guards (see section 5)
  What: create-admin shows the target host and asks for confirmation when it
  isn't localhost. The test suite and the e2e reset refuse non-localhost
  databases.
  Check: pytest: a non-localhost TEST_DATABASE_URL aborts; the e2e reset refuses

  Neon-style hosts; create-admin won't proceed without a "yes" for remote
  hosts.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task: T7 Secret check before commit
  What: A git hook (opt-in) and scripts/check-secrets.sh that block staged files

  containing neon.tech, postgresql://…:…@ with a non-local host, JWT_SECRET=
  with a value, or JWT-shaped strings.
  Check: The script catches a planted test string in a temporary file, and
  passes
  on the current repo.

  ---

  5. Keeping sample data out of the live database

  - Live starts empty by construction. Neon is a new database. On first start,
    migrations create only the schema. There's no seed script, and none will be
    added.
  - Local data stays local. Your local .env keeps pointing at localhost. The
    Neon address is never written into .env or any file, and is only typed in
    your Terminal for step E.
  - Guards in code (T6):
    - the test suite refuses any non-localhost database;
    - the e2e reset refuses anything not named *_e2e and anything not on
      localhost;
    - create-admin shows which database host it will write to and asks for
      confirmation.
  - Never run against live: the Playwright end-to-end test, my command-line
    check scripts, and the sample-data commands I used locally.
  - Files that ship: the e2e fixture PDFs are in the repo but are only ever
    uploaded to tender_desk_e2e. uploads/ and local database volumes are ignored
    by git and never deployed.
  - Proof after deploy:
    - the live GET /api/tenders?status=all returns total: 0 before your first
      real upload;
    - Neon's dashboard (Tables view) shows admins: 1, tenders: 0 and
      tender_pdfs: 0.

  ---

  6. Final go-live check

  From my side (public URLs only, read-only):
  - /api/health returns OK.
  - /docs returns a 404.
  - /api/tenders returns 200 with total: 0.
  - /api/admin/tenders without a token returns 401.
  - A 6th login attempt within a minute returns 429.
  - Requests from your Vercel address are allowed and from other addresses are
    not.
  - /admin and /tenders/1 load on Vercel (deep links work).

  From your phone, on mobile data (Wi-Fi off, to prove it's public):
  1. Open the Vercel address. The first load may show "Starting up the server…"
     for up to a minute, then "No tenders yet."
  2. Open /admin. It redirects to login. A wrong password shows the error
     message.
  3. Log in, click Add tender, upload one real public tender PDF (D4) with its
     real source URL, fill in the fields, and Publish.
  4. Log out. The tender is on the list with Open or Closing soon. Search finds
     it. The detail page shows the notice, and "View original document" opens
     the source.
  5. Open /admin again. It sends you to login.
  6. Rotate to landscape and back. There's no sideways scrolling.

  Afterwards:
  - Neon shows 1 admin, 1 tender and 1 PDF of the right size.
  - I check the Render log you share has no errors and doesn't contain the
    secrets.

          +ect while there is a single server, and the counts reset when

          + proxy's forwarded header.                                    
      130 +- All traffic over HTTPS in production (provided by the hostin

         +der (free web service), database on Neon (free plan). This is a
         + non-commercial course project, which the Vercel Hobby plan req
         +uires. Moving to AWS like Musafir is possible later. See `deplo
         +yment-spec.md`.                                                
      62  - **AI:** Claude API for field extraction. The API key is read 
          on the server only and never sent to the browser.
      63  - **Data:** about 20 real, publicly published, text-based tende
          r PDFs collected by hand for v1.
      64  - **Source documents are authoritative.** Every tender keeps it

      4  
      5  ## 1. Problem Statement
      6  Indian government RFIs and RFPs for drones and counter-drone sys
         tems are published in many separate places: the Ministry of Defe
         nce website, the GeM portal, the Central Public Procurement Port
         al, state police websites, PSUs and research institutes. Each on
         e is a long PDF in a different format. A drone or anti-drone OEM
          today has to check all these sites by hand, then read every doc
         ument to find the few facts that decide whether to bid: what is 
         being bought, how many, the key technical requirements, who is e
         ligible, and when bids close. Opportunities are missed and readi
         ng time is wasted.

       5  ## 1. General Rules
       6 -- **Base path:** all endpoints start with `/api`. The server ad
         -dress comes from `VITE_API_BASE_URL` in the frontend, which doe
         -s not include `/api` (for example `http://localhost:8000`).    
       6 +- **Base path:** all endpoints start with `/api`. The server ad
         +dress comes from `VITE_API_BASE_URL` in the frontend, which doe
         +s not include `/api` (for example `http://localhost:8000` local
         +ly, or the Render service address in production). It is set per
         + environment and never hardcoded.                              
       7 +- **Cross-origin requests** are accepted only from the frontend
         + addresses in the backend's `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`.                 
       8  - **Format:** requests and responses are JSON, except the PDF u
          pload, which is `multipart/form-data`.
       9  - **Dates** (calendar days) are strings in `YYYY-MM-DD` form, f
          or example `"2026-11-14"`.
      10  - **Timestamps** are ISO 8601 strings in India time (`+05:30`),
           for example `"2026-10-12T22:30:00+05:30"`.

⏺ P0 is done and committed on a new deploy branch as 0d8940c. It changes only
  the specs, and nothing is pushed yet. I branched off main the same way as for
  slice 1, so this stage can merge back through a pull request.

  What changed in the specs:
  - goal-spec.md §8: hosting is now Vercel Hobby, Render free and Neon free, at
    zero cost. It records that this is a non-commercial course project (your
    D1). AWS is a possible later move.
  - backend-spec.md:
    - PDFs are stored in the database, in a new TenderPdf table with the file,
      its size and its SHA-256 fingerprint. A tender and its PDF are saved
      together or not at all, and pdf_storage_key is removed.
    - PDF viewing links are signed by the backend instead of S3. The PDF view
      itself still isn't built.
    - Rate limits return 429, and are counted in the server's memory.
    - In production, the API docs pages are off, and the server refuses to start
      without proper settings.
    - Settings: UPLOAD_DIR and S3_BUCKET_NAME are removed, ENABLE_API_DOCS is
      added, and a plain postgresql:// address is accepted.
    - Daily backups are recorded as a known gap.
  - api-contract.md: the backend address is set per environment, and only the
    frontend's own address may call the API. The signed PDF link endpoint and
    the rate-limit thresholds are written in.
  - frontend-spec.md:
    - every route serves the app, so deep links work on Vercel;
    - after 5 seconds of loading, "Starting up the server. This can take up to a
      minute." appears;
    - a login refused for too many attempts gets its own message.
  - New deployment-spec.md:
    - the hosting map, Singapore region and free-plan behaviour;
    - how the app is built and run;
    - which settings are secret and which are public, with the rules for
      handling secrets;
    - the empty live database and how the admin is created;
    - the manual backup (your D2): pg_dump from the live database, restored into
      a separate local scratch database, never into the live one, with a known
      tender's details and PDF fingerprint checked to match;
    - the go-live acceptance checks.

  Check results: only specs/ changed, in one commit, and all four existing specs
  are now Draft v0.3. The only remaining S3 and AWS mentions are the intended
  "possible later move" notes.

  Updated task list:
  - T1 PDFs in the database.
  - T2 Production settings.
  - T3 Rate limits.
  - T4 Frontend hosting changes.
  - T5 Render start script.
  - T6 Live-data guards.
  - T7 Secret check before commits.
  - T8 Docs.
  - T9 Deploy: your steps with the three hosting providers, then the go-live
    checks.
  - T10 (new, from D2): the first manual pg_dump backup and the restore proof,
    written up in the README.

  Next is T1, storing PDFs in the database. Shall I go ahead?

⏺ T1 is committed on deploy as 30344ea. It isn't pushed yet, so the branch is
  one commit ahead of GitHub. Uploaded PDFs are now stored in the database
  instead of on disk.

  What changed:
  - New tender_pdfs table: one PDF per tender, with the file itself, its size
    and its SHA-256 fingerprint. The tender, its PDF and the extraction record
    are saved together or not at all, so the old clean-up-the-orphan-file code
    is gone.
  - Pages never load PDF bytes: the file content isn't fetched unless code
    explicitly asks for it, so lists and tender pages never load megabytes of
    PDF.
  - Migration 0002: adds the table and drops the old pdf_storage_key column.
  - Removed: the disk storage code, plus UPLOAD_DIR and S3_BUCKET_NAME from the
    code, .env.example, the README, CLAUDE.md and the Playwright config.

  Check results:
  - Backend: 173 tests pass, including three new ones:
    - an upload is stored byte-for-byte with the correct size and fingerprint;
    - a full 20 MB file is stored intact, and 20 MB + 1 byte is still refused
      with a 413;
    - a failure after the PDF is added saves neither the tender nor the PDF.
  - No PDF bytes on ordinary requests: a fourth test confirms that listing,
    reading, editing, publishing and the public pages never query tender_pdfs.
    To make sure it can fail, I temporarily made tenders always load their PDF,
    and the test failed.
  - Migration: applied to your dev database, it round-trips, and alembic check
    reports that the models match.
  - End-to-end: the browser journey passes 2/2 against a database built from
    scratch. The PDFs stored in the database are byte-for-byte identical to the
    fixture file (630 bytes, same fingerprint).
  - Frontend: 100 tests pass and lint is clean.

  Things to know:
  - Your 3 local sample tenders: they're kept but no longer have files, as
    agreed in D5. Your local .env still has an UPLOAD_DIR= line; the app now
    ignores it, and you can delete it whenever you like.
  - One test of mine was broken at first: it recorded no database queries at
    all, because it was listening on a second copy of the test setup. Its own
    "should have run queries" check caught that before it could pass for the
    wrong reason. I fixed it before committing.
  - Dev backend restarted: the old process was still running the previous code,
    which would have failed against the new table layout. It's running the new
    code now, and the frontend is still on 5173.

  Next is T2, production settings: accepting Neon's postgresql:// address,
  turning the API docs off in production, and refusing to start with unsafe
  settings. Shall I push deploy and go on?

⏺ Deploy pushed—now moving to T2, production settings. I spotted that pydantic's
  error messages leak rejected values (e.g., a too-short JWT_SECRET could print
  in full to Render's logs), so I'll make startup errors name the setting and
  issue without ever exposing the value.

      15 +def normalize_database_url(url: str) -> str:                   
      16 +    """Accepts a plain postgres:// or postgresql:// address, as
         + hosting providers give it.                                    
      17 +                                                               
      18 +    SQLAlchemy reads a bare "postgresql://" as "use psycopg2", 
         +which is not installed,                                        
      19 +    so the scheme is pointed at psycopg (v3). Everything after 
         +the scheme, including                                          
      20 +    options such as ?sslmode=require, is kept unchanged.       
      21 +    """                                                        
      22 +    scheme, sep, rest = url.partition("://")                   
      23 +    if not sep:                                                
      24 +        raise ValueError("must look like postgresql://user:pass
         +word@host/database")                                           
      25 +    if scheme in ("postgres", "postgresql"):                   
      26 +        scheme = PSYCOPG_SCHEME                                
      27 +    if scheme != PSYCOPG_SCHEME:                               
      28 +        raise ValueError(f"must be a PostgreSQL address (postgr
         +esql:// or {PSYCOPG_SCHEME}://)")                              
      29 +    return f"{scheme}://{rest}"                                
      30 +

⏺ Update(CLAUDE.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line
      52  - **Never invent extracted values.** Anything that can't be val
          idated stays `null` and goes in `fields_not_found`.
      53  - **Secrets** (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `JWT_SECRET`, passwords) ar
          e never logged, committed, or sent to the browser. The frontend
           reads only `VITE_API_BASE_URL`, which excludes `/api`.
      54  - Admin endpoints check the JWT on the server every time. Hidin
          g links in the UI is only a convenience.
      55 +- **Settings** are validated at startup (`backend/app/config.py
         +`). A bad or missing setting stops the server with one line per
         + problem that names the variable but **never prints its value**
         +; keep it that way when adding settings. `DATABASE_URL` may be 
         +a plain `postgresql://` address (the `psycopg` driver is added 
         +automatically).

       1 +#!/usr/bin/env bash                                            
       2 +# Start command for the Render web service (deployment-spec §3)
         +. Run from backend/:                                           
       3 +#   bash render-start.sh                                       
       4 +#                                                              
       5 +# 1. Bring the database schema up to date. Render's free plan h
         +as no shell or                                                 
       6 +#    one-off jobs, so migrations run here, on every start; when
         + nothing is new                                                
       7 +#    this is a quick no-op. If a migration fails, the server do
         +es not start.                                                  
       8 +# 2. Start the API on the port Render gives us.                
       9 +#                                                              
      10 +# --no-proxy-headers: the app reads the visitor's address itsel
         +f, using                                                       
      11 +# TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS (app/ratelimit.py), so uvicorn must not re
         +write it.

  clean, and the e2e journey passes 2/2.

  Bugs found and fixed along the way:
  - Startup errors could leak secrets: pydantic's error message would have
    printed a short JWT_SECRET into Render's logs. Fixed in T2.
  - create-admin crashed at its prompts: Ctrl-D or Ctrl-C produced a Python
    traceback; it now cancels cleanly. Found by a real-terminal check in T6.
  - The secret check had a false alarm: it flagged the local IPv6 address [::1].
    Fixed in T7.
  - The README's backup comparison was wrong: it read 0 rows from the live side
    because -e Q passed nothing. It failed loudly rather than wrongly passing,
    and was found by rehearsing the exact command in T8.

  Needs deciding before T9:
  1. deploy must reach main first. The README tells Render and Vercel to deploy
     from main, but all of this work is only on deploy. I'd open a pull request
     from deploy to main and merge it, like slice 1.
  2. The Render IP header is a measurement, not a guess. Render's documentation
     doesn't settle how it passes on a visitor's address, so TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS
     starts at 1 and the go-live check confirms it. That check is three things:
     5 wrong logins from your laptop block a 6th, your phone on mobile data can
     still log in, and a faked header from the laptop is still blocked.
  3. Two other things are only verifiable live: whether Render's build picks up
     uv as its docs say, and how Render's "waking up" page appears to the
     frontend. If either surprises us, I'll fix it during T9.

  Two small notes:
  - Leftover .env line: your local .env still has an UPLOAD_DIR= line. It's
    harmless and ignored; delete it whenever you like.
  - Hook in other clones: the secret check only runs in a clone where bash 
    scripts/install-git-hooks.sh has been run. This clone has it.

  Sources (Render forwarded-IP question):
  - Render feedback: Send the correct X_FORWARDED_FOR
    (https://feedback.render.com/features/p/send-the-correct-xforwardedfor)
  - Render community: missing X-Forwarded-For on API rewrites
    (https://community.render.com/t/missing-x-forwarded-for-header-on-api-rewrit
    es-to-backend/24481)
  - Render docs: Python version (https://render.com/docs/python-version)

⏺ What T2 to T8 changed, in plain language

  - T2, safe settings. The backend accepts the database address exactly as Neon
    gives it. It can hide its technical documentation pages in production, and
    only accepts calls from the exact website address you list. If a setting is
    wrong or missing, it refuses to start and says which one, without ever
    printing a password or secret into the logs.
  - T3, rate limits. Someone guessing passwords gets 5 tries per minute, then
    must wait. Each visitor can make 60 requests per minute to the public pages,
    which stops anyone hammering the site. Visitors can't get around the limit
    by pretending to be someone else.
  - T4, ready for Vercel. Links like /admin or /tenders/12 work when typed in or
    reloaded. If the free backend is asleep, after 5 seconds the page says
    "Starting up the server. This can take up to a minute." instead of looking
    stuck. If the host returns an unexpected page instead of data, the site
    shows an error with Retry rather than an empty list.
  - T5, how Render starts the app. One script updates the database structure,
    then starts the backend. A small file tells Render to use Python 3.12.
  - T6, protecting the live database. The automated tests and the e2e test
    refuse to touch any database that isn't on your computer. When you create
    the admin account, the tool shows which database it's about to change and
    makes you type yes if it's the live one.
  - T7, secret alarm. Before every commit, a check looks for passwords, secret
    keys, tokens and database addresses, and blocks the commit if it finds one.
  - T8, instructions. The README now has step-by-step deployment instructions
    and a tested backup-and-restore procedure. CLAUDE.md has the rules for
    working with the live site.

  All tests pass, re-run just now:
  - 263 backend tests;
  - 104 frontend tests, with a clean build and lint;
  - 2 end-to-end browser tests;
  - a clean secret scan.

  Region and merge

  - Region (D3): the spec and README now say Ohio for both Neon and Render, so
    the backend and database sit together. Visitors in India will see about a
    quarter of a second extra per request, while pages themselves still load
    from Vercel's nearby servers.
  - Merged: pull request #2
    (https://github.com/vijayjalneela/drone-tender-desk/pull/2) is merged into

  T9, step 1 of 6: check your Neon project and copy the right connection string

  You've already created the Neon project, so this step only checks a few
  settings and copies one value.

  1. Open console.neon.tech and click your project.
  2. On the project dashboard, click the Connect button near the top right. A
     box opens.
  3. In that box:
     - Branch: leave it on main (or production, whichever is the default).
     - Database: note the name shown. The README suggested tender_desk, but
       Neon's default neondb works just as well.
     - Connection pooling: make sure this switch is off.
  4. With pooling off, look at the connection string. It starts postgresql://,
     and the part after @ should not contain -pooler.
  5. Click the copy icon and save the string in your password manager as "Neon
     DATABASE_URL".

  - the database name (tender_desk or neondb);

  - confirmed: the saved string has no -pooler in it.
